The project choice overrides the app-wide profile scope; an explicit orca tab create --profile overrides both. Choose Browser setting to inherit the app-wide scope again. Phone-created and headless orca serve tabs do not apply this project choice.

Isolation

Each profile has its own storage partition — cookies, local storage, cache. Profiles don't leak into each other.

英文原文：English source: orca.dev/docs/browser/profiles

上一章Design Mode 下一章Bots quickstart

Bots

Bot 快速开始

Bots quickstart

建一个 Bot，交给它第一件活，排上时间表，再决定谁可以找它。

Bot 从自己的对话、Slack、Linear、GitHub 或时间表接活。活在你的电脑上做，用的是你自己的 Claude Code、Codex，或其他受支持的 Agent。做完后，它回到你提问的地方汇报。要你做决定时，它在 Orca 里等你。

这一页从没有 Bot，走到一个有第一件活、有时间表、同事也能找到的 Bot。想先看它能接什么活，见 Bot 能做的事。

每个 Bot 有名字、用途、记忆和自动化，住在某一台电脑的一个文件夹里。

开始之前

  • 这台电脑上的 Orca 是最新版。
  • Claude Code、Codex 或其他受支持的 Agent 已安装，并且已在这台电脑上登录。Bot 用它来跑，用量算在那个订阅上。
  • 要从 Slack、GitHub 或 Linear 找到 Bot：先登录 Orca。对话、时间表和 Google 不登录也能用。

创建一个 Bot

  1. 在侧栏的 Bots（Bot）一节点 +（New bot or group，新建 Bot 或分组），再选 New bot（新建 Bot）→ Start from scratch（从零开始）。想用 24 个模板之一，改选 Browse templates（浏览模板）。
  2. 给它起名字，配一张图。
  3. 在 Who it is and what it does（它是谁、做什么）里用一句话写下它的工作。这一步可以跳过，也可以留到第一次对话里说。
  4. 看清它跑在哪台电脑、用哪个 Agent。要换，点 Change（更改）。选定之后，这个 Bot 就留在那台电脑上。
  5. 点 Create（创建）。点之前什么都不会建出来。

侧栏里没有 Bots？

Bot 默认是开的。侧栏没有 Bots 时，到 Settings → Bots 打开 Enable bots（启用 Bot）。关掉之后，Bot 会藏起来，新消息和新的自动化也不再跑。文件夹、记忆和对话还在原地。

交给它第一件活

从零开始的 Bot 会先打招呼，问你第一件做什么。用一件真事回答，例如：

  • 「每天早上，总结 acme/web 有什么变化，以及哪些要我看。」
  • 「PR 上的 CI 失败时，告诉我是偶发还是真问题。」

Bot 会看这件活需要什么，先做一小块，把结果给你看。然后它写下自己是干什么的，以后每次对话都从这里开始。它把学到的放进记忆，所以每件活都可以新开一场对话，不用把背景再说一遍。

改代码时，Bot 把活交给一个 Worker：在新的工作区里开一场 Claude Code 或 Codex 会话。这个工作区是一棵 Git 工作树，改动不会进你自己的检出。Worker 出现在侧栏里这个 Bot 下面，PR 会回到 Bot 这里。

模板自带用途；有的还带能力和时间表。时间表一开始是关的。你选定仓库或频道之后，自动化和连接器会在 Bot 的第一次对话里配好。

跟它说话

在侧栏点这个 Bot，打开对话，直接问。在 Orca 的任意对话里输入 @，可以提到一个 Bot，把它拉进这场对话。

Bot 需要你做决定、登录或批准时，会说出来。这条请求停在 Bot 的 Home（主页）里的 Needs you（需要你处理），直到你回答。可以选它建议的回复，也可以自己写。

排上时间表

在对话里让它按时间做事（「每个工作日早上 9 点」），或在某件事发生时做事（「acme/web 开了 PR 时」）。它会建好自动化，并告诉你跑在哪里、结果送到哪里。自动化出现在 Bot 的面板里，可以暂停，也可以恢复。

时间表可以是每小时、每天、工作日、每周，也可以是 cron 或 RRULE。能触发 Bot 的事件，取决于你加了哪些连接器。见 连接器 和 定时自动化。

电脑得开着

Bot 只在它所在的电脑醒着、并且在线时工作。Orca 里的 Keep computer awake（防止电脑休眠）可以在 Agent 工作时避免空闲休眠。Mac 合上盖子仍然会睡。来自 Slack、GitHub 和 Linear 的请求最多等一天，等电脑回来再跑。想让 Bot 一直能被找到，把它放在已经和 Orca 配对的服务器上，并让那台服务器保持醒着。

你随时可以接手

Bot 的工作留在 Orca 里，任何一步你都可以接上：

  • 某个 Worker 的工作区： 在侧栏里点 Bot 下面的那个 Worker。你会进入它的工作树，编辑器、终端和浏览器都停在它离开的地方。
  • 来自 Slack、GitHub 或 Linear 的对话： 在 Bot 下面有自己的一场对话。Slack 里，在讨论串中找 Open this thread in Orca（在 Orca 中打开此讨论串）。Linear 里，打开议题上 Bot 的 Agent 会话，点 Open in Orca（在 Orca 中打开）。
  • 它的 PR： 和其他 PR 一样审。它们来自 Worker 的分支，不是你自己的检出。

从 Slack、GitHub 和 Linear 找到它

在 Bot 的 Connectors（连接器）标签里加一个连接器。之后大家在原来工作的地方找它：

  • Slack： 在频道里 @scout，或发私信。每个 Bot 是自己的一个 Slack 应用。
  • GitHub： 在议题或 PR 里按它的 How to ask（怎么找它）那一行去提及。
  • Linear： 在议题上写 @Orca scout（Linear 的提及走 Orca 应用），或加上标签 bot:scout。

每个 Slack 工作区要一次 App Configuration Token（应用配置令牌）。工作区也可能要管理员批准这个 Bot 的应用。连接器 里有逐步做法。

决定谁可以提问

默认只有你能问自己的 Bot。要让同事从 Slack、Linear 或 GitHub 问它，把他们加进 Who can ask（谁可以提问）。他们的请求用的是你的权限，跑在你的电脑上。只加你愿意让他们坐在你键盘前的人。不在名单上的人来问时，Orca 通常会回复一句「去问你」：Slack 上是私信，GitHub 和 Linear 上是公开回复。决定在 Orca 里由你来做。

加人之前先读 安全。如果它的 Agent 支持，考虑把 Bot 改成 Ask for approval（先问再做）。见 权限。

不用你开口也会做的两件事

除了你的提问和自动化，Bot 自己还会做两件小事。两件都用你的 Agent 订阅：

  • Look around（巡看）：一天几次，用 Claude Code 或 Codex 的 Bot 会读 Slack、Linear、GitHub 和你的项目里等着你的东西，把下一步列在 Suggestions（建议）下面。它只读，不能发消息，也不能改文件。默认是工作日早上 8 点到晚上 8 点，每 4 小时一次，并且只在 Bot 那台电脑上的 Orca 正在运行时跑。频率和开关在 Bot 设置的 Look around 里。
  • Memory upkeep（整理记忆）：默认在有新对话的那天之后，用 Claude Code 或 Codex 的 Bot 会在凌晨 3 点之后整理记忆，前提是它没被暂停、也没在忙。电脑睡着时错过的那次，醒来后可以补上。你可以看它改了什么；Undo（撤销）可用时，用它恢复 MEMORY.md。改成每周一次，或关掉，都在 Bot 设置的 Memory upkeep 里。

把已有的 Bot 带过来

如果你在别的 Bot 应用里已经有一个，在 Bots 一节点 +，再选 New bot → Import from another app…（从其他应用导入…）。新 Bot 会读取旧的那个，给你看一份计划，然后自己装好。导入会跳过密钥和登录文件。每个服务都要在 Orca 里重新连接。见 导入 Bot。

下一步

  • Bot 能做的事：八件可以交给 Bot 的活，一步一步写。
  • 连接器：从 Slack、GitHub 和 Linear 找到 Bot，并让它在 Google 里工作。
  • 导入 Bot：把另一个应用里已有的 Bot 带过来。
  • 安全：Bot 能碰到什么、谁可以问它、数据留在哪里。

Create a bot, give it a job, put it on a schedule, and decide who can ask it.

A bot takes jobs from its chat, Slack, Linear, GitHub or a schedule. It works on your computer with your own Claude Code or Codex, or another supported agent, and reports back where you asked. When it needs a decision, it waits for you in Orca.

This page gets you from no bot to one with a first job, a schedule and a way for teammates to reach it. For ideas, see Use cases.

Each bot has a name, a purpose, a memory and automations, and lives in a folder on one computer.

Before you start

  • Orca on your computer, on the latest version.
  • Claude Code, Codex or another supported agent, installed and signed in on that computer. The bot runs on it, so its work counts against that plan.
  • To reach the bot from Slack, GitHub or Linear: sign in to Orca. Chat, schedules and Google work without it.

Create a bot

  1. In the sidebar's Bots section, select + (New bot or group), then New bot → Start from scratch. To start from one of the 24 templates instead, pick Browse templates.
  2. Give it a name and a picture.
  3. In the box that says Who it is and what it does, describe its job in a sentence. This is optional; you can also tell it in its first chat.
  4. Check the line that says where it runs: which computer and which agent. Select Change to pick another. A bot stays on the computer you pick.
  5. Select Create. Nothing is created until you do.
Don't see Bots?

Bots are on by default. If the sidebar has no Bots section, turn on Settings → Bots → Enable bots. Turning bots off hides them and stops new messages and automation runs. Their folders, memory and chats stay where they are.

Give it a first job

A bot you start from scratch greets you and asks what to work on first. Answer with one real task, for example:

  • "Every morning, summarize what changed in acme/web and what needs my review."
  • "When CI fails on a pull request, tell me if it's flaky or real."

The bot looks at what that task needs, does one small piece of it right away and shows you the result. Then it writes down what it's for, so every later chat starts from the same place. It keeps what it learns in its memory, so you can start a new chat for each job without repeating yourself.

For code changes, the bot hands the work to a worker: a Claude Code or Codex session in its own new workspace (a git worktree), so its changes stay out of your checkout. Workers show up under the bot in the sidebar, and their pull requests come back to the bot.

A template comes with a purpose and, for some, abilities and schedules. Its schedules start turned off. Its automations and connectors are set up in the bot's first chat, once you pick the repo or channel.

Talk to it

Select the bot in the sidebar to open its chat and ask it anything. In any Orca chat, type @ to mention a bot and bring it into the conversation.

When the bot needs you, for a decision, a sign-in or an approval, it says so, and the request waits under Needs you on the bot's Home until you answer. Pick one of the replies it suggests, or answer in your own words.

Put it on a schedule

Ask the bot in chat to do something on a schedule ("every weekday at 9") or when something happens ("when a pull request is opened in acme/web"). It sets up the automation and tells you where it runs and where the results go. The automation shows in the bot's panel, where you can pause or resume it.

Schedules can be hourly, daily, weekdays, weekly, cron or RRULE. The events a bot can start on depend on the connectors you've added. See Connectors and Scheduled automations.

The computer has to be on

A bot works while the computer it lives on is awake and online. The Keep computer awake setting in Orca prevents idle sleep while agents work; on a Mac, closing the lid still sleeps it. Requests from Slack, GitHub and Linear wait up to a day for the computer to come back. To keep a bot reachable, run it on a server you've paired with Orca and keep that server awake.

Step in

A bot's work stays in Orca, so you can pick it up at any point:

  • A worker's workspace: select the worker under the bot in the sidebar. You get its worktree with the editor, terminal and browser, where the worker left off.
  • A conversation from Slack, GitHub or Linear: it has its own chat under the bot. In Slack, look for the Open this thread in Orca link in the thread. In Linear, open the bot's agent session on the issue and select Open in Orca.
  • Its pull requests: review them like any other. They come from a worker's branch, not from your checkout.

Reach it from Slack, GitHub and Linear

Add a connector from the bot's Connectors tab. Then people ask it where they already work:

  • Slack: @scout in a channel, or a direct message. Each bot is its own Slack app.
  • GitHub: a mention on an issue or pull request, as the bot's How to ask line shows.
  • Linear: @Orca scout on an issue (Linear mentions go through the Orca app), or the label bot:scout.

Slack needs a one-time App Configuration Token per workspace. Your workspace may also need an admin to approve the bot's app. Connectors walks through it.

Decide who can ask

By default, only you can ask your bot. To let teammates ask it from Slack, Linear or GitHub, add them under Who can ask. Their requests run on your access, on your computer, so add only people you'd trust at your keyboard. When someone who isn't on the list asks, Orca normally replies with a note to ask you: privately on Slack, publicly on GitHub and Linear. You decide in Orca.

Before you add anyone, read Security. Consider switching the bot to Ask for approval, if its agent supports it; see Permissions.

What runs without you asking

Besides your asks and automations, a bot does two small jobs on its own. Both use your agent's plan:

  • Look around: a few times a day, a bot on Claude Code or Codex reads what's waiting for you in Slack, Linear, GitHub and your projects, and lists next steps under Suggestions. It is read-only: it can't post or change files. By default it is scheduled every 4 hours from 8 AM to 8 PM on weekdays, and runs while Orca is running on the bot's computer. Change how often, or turn it off, under Look around in the bot's settings.
  • Memory upkeep: by default, after a day with new chats, a bot on Claude Code or Codex tidies its memory that night after 3 AM, when it isn't paused or busy. A run missed while the computer was asleep can catch up once it wakes. You can review the changes, and restore MEMORY.md with Undo when it's available. Switch it to once a week, or turn it off, under Memory upkeep in the bot's settings.

Bring over a bot you already have

If you run a bot in another bot app, select + in the Bots section, then New bot → Import from another app…. Your new bot reads the old one, shows you a plan and sets itself up. Import skips key and login files, and you reconnect each service in Orca. See Import a bot.

Next steps

  • Use cases: eight jobs to hand a bot, step by step.
  • Connectors: reach your bot from Slack, GitHub and Linear, and let it work in Google.
  • Import a bot: bring over a bot you already have in another app.
  • Security: what a bot can reach, who can ask it, and where its data stays.

英文原文：English source: orca.dev/docs/bots

上一章Browser-use profiles 下一章Bot use cases

Bots

Bot 能做的事

Bot use cases

下面八件活，现在就可以交给 Bot。每件都写清：从哪里开口、它做什么、结果回到哪里、你在哪里接手。

每一件都建立在 Orca 自带的模板或连接器上，形状相同：你在 Slack、Linear、GitHub、它的对话里，或按时间表开口；Bot 在你的电脑上做；它回到你提问的地方汇报；该由人决定的地方，它等你。

例子里用演示 Bot Scout、演示仓库 acme/web 和 Slack 频道 #eng。换成你自己的即可。

跳到一件活：

  • 修别人在 Slack 里报的 bug
  • 给新的 Linear 议题分诊
  • 盯着 CI，修掉失败的检查
  • 工作日早上的站会
  • 有人请你时再审 PR
  • 起草发布说明
  • 让文档跟着代码走
  • 响应 webhook
  • 更多模板

开始之前

  • 按 Bot 快速开始 配好一个 Bot。用 + → New bot（新建 Bot）→ Browse templates（浏览模板）从模板开始，或从零开始，在第一次对话里描述这件活。
  • 每件活点名的连接器，从 Bot 的 Connectors（连接器）标签加上。见 连接器。
  • 模板的时间表一开始是关的。你告诉它用哪个仓库或频道之后，在 Bot 的面板里打开。

修别人在 Slack 里报的 bug

从这里开始： 模板 Bug investigation（查 bug），接上 GitHub；再接 Slack，别人就能在那里问。

  • 怎么开口。 在 #eng 里，Maya 写：「@scout Safari 上结账坏了，你看看？」
  • Bot 做什么。 它在一棵新的 Git 工作树里启动 Worker，复现这个 bug，写下步骤，再找到从哪里开始。写修复之前它会先问。你同意之后，Worker 去修，跑测试，并用你的 git 登录开一个 PR。
  • 回到哪里。 Slack 讨论串里一条回复：原因、测试结果和 PR。
  • 你在哪里接手。 「Start a fix?」（开始修吗？）停在 Needs you（需要你处理）。PR 开出来之后，和其他 PR 一样审。想看近一点，从 Orca 侧栏里 Bot 下面打开那个 Worker：你会进到它的工作树，带着编辑器、终端和浏览器。

在 Slack 讨论串里找 Open this thread in Orca（在 Orca 中打开此讨论串）。它打开的是 Orca 里的同一场对话。

给新的 Linear 议题分诊

从这里开始： 一个接了 Linear 的 Bot。这件事没有模板，在对话里描述。

  • 怎么开口。 在 Bot 的对话里：「Linear 里 ENG 团队来了新议题时，检查有没有复现步骤，找重复项，并建议标签和负责人。关掉任何东西之前先问我。」
  • Bot 做什么。 它建一条自动化，由 Linear 的新议题事件触发。每条新议题它会读描述，搜索可能的重复项，并按你允许的范围、以你的身份做修改。
  • 回到哪里。 Bot 的对话里，每条议题一行短话：它发现了什么、改了什么。
  • 你在哪里接手。 你说过要问的事，例如关掉一条重复议题，停在 Needs you。别人也可以把单条议题交给它。Linear 里的提及走 Orca 应用，所以他们在议题上写 @Orca scout（不是 @scout），或加上标签 bot:scout。

盯着 CI，修掉失败的检查

从这里开始： 模板 CI failures（CI 失败），接上 GitHub。

  • 怎么开口。 模板已经写好：「检查失败时，说它是偶发还是真问题。」在对话里再加一句：「如果是真的，开始修之前先问我。」
  • Bot 做什么。 你选定的仓库里有检查失败时，它读失败内容，解释原因，并判断看起来是偶发还是真问题。它会留一份偶发检查清单，周一早上 9 点发出。
  • 回到哪里。 解释出现在 Bot 的对话里。
  • 你在哪里接手。 真失败时，它在 Needs you 里问要不要启动一个修 bug 的 Worker。你说要，修复会以 PR 回来，给你审。

工作日早上的站会

从这里开始： 模板 Daily standup notes（每日站会笔记），接上 GitHub 和 Linear。想发到 Slack，再接 Slack。

  • 怎么开口。 模板已经写好：工作日早上 9 点。在 Bot 的面板里打开这个时间表。想分享的话，在对话里加：「也发到 #standup。」
  • Bot 做什么。 它看你的工作区、PR 和议题，写下你和你的 Agent 昨天做了什么、接下来做什么。
  • 回到哪里。 笔记在 Bot 的对话里。你要求的话，也会以这个 Bot 自己的 Slack 应用发到 #standup。
  • 你在哪里接手。 说错的地方在对话里纠正。它把学到的放进记忆，第二天的笔记从那里接着写。

时间表只在 Bot 那台电脑醒着时跑。见 排上时间表。

有人请你时再审 PR

从这里开始： 模板 Pull request review（PR 审阅），接上 GitHub。

  • 怎么开口。 模板已经写好：有人在你选定的仓库里请你审一个 PR 时。
  • Bot 做什么。 它审这个 PR，列出有风险的改动和缺的测试，并标出文件和行。
  • 回到哪里。 发现写在 Bot 的对话里，例如：「#4121：重试计数从未复位，所以第二次故障会停掉重试。」
  • 你在哪里接手。 你允许之前，它不会在 GitHub 上评论。你告诉它发哪几条，它就以你的身份发出。它的规则是：只有你开口，才去批准、要求修改或合并。见 Orca 实际执行的部分。

起草发布说明

从这里开始： 模板 Release notes（发布说明），接上 GitHub。

  • 怎么开口。 在 Bot 的对话里：「起草 4.12 的说明。」接了 Slack 的话，也可以在频道里问：「@scout 起草 4.12 的说明。」
  • Bot 做什么。 它读上次发布之后合并的 PR，按三类给用户写说明：新增、修复、变更。
  • 回到哪里。 草稿出现在你提问的讨论串或对话里。
  • 你在哪里接手。 在对话里改措辞。读起来对了，让它开一个 PR，把说明加进更新日志，或你自己复制到发布的地方。

让文档跟着代码走

从这里开始： 模板 Docs upkeep（文档维护），接上 GitHub。

  • 怎么开口。 模板已经写好：你选定的仓库，周三上午 10 点。
  • Bot 做什么。 它拿最近合并的 PR 对文档，列出已经对不上的页面，并标出现在错了的那一行。
  • 回到哪里。 清单在 Bot 的对话里。
  • 你在哪里接手。 挑出要改的页面。Worker 在自己的工作树里改，并开一个 PR 给你审。

响应 webhook

从这里开始： 模板 Deploy watch（盯部署），接上 GitHub，这样它能读每次部署带上的 PR。任何能发网页请求的工具，都可以用同样的方式启动 Bot。

  • 怎么开口。 在 Bot 的对话里：「给我们的部署设一个 webhook。」在自动化卡片上用 Copy URL（复制 URL）复制地址，贴进你的部署工具。工具支持的话，再加上一个签名密钥。
  • Bot 做什么。 工具每次调用，它按这次部署带上的 PR 做摘要，并标出失败的部署。Orca 把这次调用带来的内容交给它，当作资料，不当作指令。
  • 回到哪里。 每次部署之后，摘要出现在 Bot 的对话里。
  • 你在哪里接手。 看起来不对的，再问它。URL 泄漏了，在这个 webhook 上用 Rotate key…（轮换密钥…）。见 Webhook。

先汇报，再动手

多数模板先报告发现了什么，改任何东西之前会问。你可以在对话里放宽它自己能做的范围。它的提问停在 Needs you，直到你回答。Orca 强制执行什么、只是告诉 Bot 什么，读 安全。

更多模板

Orca 自带 24 个模板。上面那些之外，还有：

模板 它做什么 什么时候汇报
Bug report intake 为 Slack 频道里的每条 bug 起草一条带复现步骤的 Linear 议题，你批准后才提交 有人报 bug 时
GitHub issue triage 为每条新议题提议标签和重复项，并列出缺的细节 议题打开时
Support triage 列出你选定的 Slack 频道里还没人答的问题，并注明哪些已经有 Linear 议题 工作日 9 点到 17 点，每 2 小时
Feedback digest 把 Slack 里的反馈按主题归组，附上原话和次数 周一早上 9 点
Backlog cleanup 列出陈旧议题、可能的重复项，以及没有负责人的议题 每月 1 日
Dependency updates 列出过时和有漏洞的包，最急的在前；你同意后，每一组一个 PR 周一早上 9 点
Security check 检查已合并的改动里有没有泄漏的机密、注入、缺认证和不安全的默认值 周一早上 9 点，以及 PR 合并时
Release readiness 留一份短清单：失败的检查、等审的 PR、还开着的阻碍 工作日早上
Incident notes 在事故频道里记时间线，然后起草一份不追责的事后记录 事故期间和之后
On-call handoff 总结这一周的告警和事故，并列出还没做的跟进 周五下午 4 点
Replies you owe 找出 Slack 和 Linear 里问了你还没回的，以及你说过要做的事 每天下午 4 点
Email digest 列出需要你回复的邮件；从不发信 工作日早上 9 点和下午 2 点
Meeting prep 列出一场会议涉及的议题、PR 和文档 每场会议前 15 分钟
Project briefing 写下变了什么、卡在哪里、要谁做决定 周五下午 3 点
Spec drafting 把一条 Slack 讨论串或一份文档收成一页规格 你开口时
Unreviewed agent work 列出已经做完、但还没有审阅或 PR 的 Agent 工作 工作日早上 9 点
Workspace cleanup 列出已完成或已放弃的工作区；只删你挑中的 周五下午 4 点

自己写一件

好的第一句开口要写清四件事：

  1. 何时： 一个时间表（「每个工作日早上 9 点」）、一个事件（「acme/web 里检查失败时」），或就是「现在」。
  2. 做什么： 一件活，一句话。
  3. 报到哪里： Bot 的对话、一个 Slack 频道，或它被问到的那条议题或 PR。
  4. 什么要问你： 动手之前它应该问什么。

例如：「acme/web 开了 PR 时，检查每个修复是不是都有测试。哪些没有，到 #eng 告诉我。不要在 GitHub 上评论。」

下一步

  • Bot 快速开始：创建一个 Bot，并交给它第一件活。
  • 连接器：接上 Slack、GitHub、Linear、Google 和 webhook。
  • 安全：Bot 能碰到什么，以及谁可以问它。

Eight jobs you can hand a bot today: where you ask, what it does, what comes back and where you step in.

Each walk-through below is a job a bot can do today, built on a template or a connector Orca ships. Every one follows the same shape: you ask (in Slack, Linear, GitHub, its chat or on a schedule), the bot works on your computer, it reports where you asked, and it waits for you where a person should decide.

The examples use a demo bot called Scout, a demo repo acme/web and a Slack channel #eng. Swap in your own.

Jump to a job:

  • Fix a bug someone reports in Slack
  • Triage new Linear issues
  • Watch CI and fix a failing check
  • A weekday standup on a schedule
  • Review a pull request when you're asked
  • Draft release notes
  • Keep docs in sync with the code
  • Respond to a webhook
  • More templates

Before you start

  • A bot set up as in the Bots quickstart. Start one from a template with + → New bot → Browse templates, or start from scratch and describe the job in its first chat.
  • The connectors each job names, added from the bot's Connectors tab. See Connectors.
  • A template's schedules start turned off. Turn them on from the bot's panel once you've told it which repo or channel to use.

Fix a bug someone reports in Slack

Start from: the Bug investigation template, with GitHub connected, plus Slack so people can ask there.

  • The ask. In #eng, Maya writes: "@scout checkout breaks on Safari, can you look?"
  • What the bot does. It starts a worker in a new git worktree that reproduces the bug and writes down the steps, then finds where it starts. It asks before it writes a fix. Once you agree, a worker fixes it, runs the tests and opens a pull request with your git login.
  • What comes back. A reply in the Slack thread: the cause, the test result and the pull request.
  • Where you step in. The "Start a fix?" question waits under Needs you. When the pull request is up, review it as you would any other. To look closer, open the worker from under the bot in Orca's sidebar: you land in its worktree with the editor, terminal and browser.

In Slack, look for the Open this thread in Orca link in the thread. It opens the same conversation in Orca.

Triage new Linear issues

Start from: a bot with Linear connected. There's no template for this one; describe it in chat.

  • The ask. In the bot's chat: "When a new issue is filed for the ENG team in Linear, check it has steps to reproduce, look for duplicates, and suggest a label and an owner. Ask me before you close anything."
  • What the bot does. It sets up an automation that starts on Linear's new-issue event. For each new issue it reads the description, searches for likely duplicates, and makes the changes you said it may make, as you.
  • What comes back. A short line per issue in the bot's chat: what it found and what it changed.
  • Where you step in. Anything you said it should ask about, such as closing a duplicate, waits under Needs you. People can also hand it a single issue. In Linear the mention goes through the Orca app, so they write @Orca scout (not @scout) on the issue, or add the label bot:scout.

Watch CI and fix a failing check

Start from: the CI failures template, with GitHub connected.

  • The ask. Set up by the template: "When a check fails, say whether it's flaky or real." Add to it in chat: "If it's real, ask me before you start a fix."
  • What the bot does. When a check fails in a repo you picked, it reads the failure, explains it, and says whether it looks flaky or real. It keeps a list of flaky checks and posts it Mondays at 9 AM.
  • What comes back. The explanation in the bot's chat.
  • Where you step in. For a real failure, it asks under Needs you whether to start a fix worker. Say yes, and the fix comes back as a pull request for you to review.

A weekday standup on a schedule

Start from: the Daily standup notes template, with GitHub and Linear connected, plus Slack if you want it posted there.

  • The ask. Set up by the template: weekdays at 9 AM. Turn the schedule on in the bot's panel. To share it, add in chat: "Also post it in #standup."
  • What the bot does. It looks at your workspaces, pull requests and issues, and writes what you and your agents did yesterday and what's next.
  • What comes back. The notes in the bot's chat, and in #standup as the bot's own Slack app if you asked.
  • Where you step in. Correct anything it got wrong in chat. It keeps what it learns in its memory, so the next day's notes start from there.

A schedule runs only while the bot's computer is awake. See Put it on a schedule.

Review a pull request when you're asked

Start from: the Pull request review template, with GitHub connected.

  • The ask. Set up by the template: when someone requests your review on a pull request in the repos you pick.
  • What the bot does. It reviews the pull request and lists risky changes and missing tests, with file and line.
  • What comes back. The findings in the bot's chat, for example: "#4121: the retry counter is never reset, so a second outage stops retries."
  • Where you step in. It doesn't comment on GitHub until you say it can. Tell it which findings to post, and it posts them as you. Its rules say approve, request changes or merge only when you ask; see what Orca enforces.

Draft release notes

Start from: the Release notes template, with GitHub connected.

  • The ask. In the bot's chat: "Draft the notes for 4.12." With Slack connected, you can ask in a channel instead: "@scout draft the notes for 4.12."
  • What the bot does. It reads the pull requests merged since the last release and writes notes for users in three groups: new, fixed and changed.
  • What comes back. The draft, in the thread or chat where you asked.
  • Where you step in. Edit the wording in chat. When it reads right, ask it to open a pull request that adds the notes to your changelog, or copy them where you publish.

Keep docs in sync with the code

Start from: the Docs upkeep template, with GitHub connected.

  • The ask. Set up by the template: Wednesdays at 10 AM, for the repos you pick.
  • What the bot does. It checks recently merged pull requests against your docs and lists the pages that no longer match, with the line that is now wrong.
  • What comes back. The list in the bot's chat.
  • Where you step in. Pick the pages to fix. A worker updates them in its own worktree and opens a pull request for you to review.

Respond to a webhook

Start from: the Deploy watch template, with GitHub connected so it can read the pull requests each deploy shipped. Any tool that can send a web request can start a bot the same way.

  • The ask. In the bot's chat: "Set up a webhook for our deploys." Copy the address with Copy URL on the automation's card and paste it into your deploy tool. Add a signing secret if the tool supports one.
  • What the bot does. Each time the tool calls, it summarizes the deploy from the pull requests it shipped and flags a deploy that failed. Orca hands it what the call carries as information, not as instructions.
  • What comes back. A summary in the bot's chat after each deploy.
  • Where you step in. Ask about anything that looks wrong. If the URL leaks, use Rotate key… on the webhook. See Webhooks.
It reports first, then acts

Most templates report what they found and ask before they change anything. You can widen what a bot does on its own in chat. Its asks wait under Needs you until you answer. To see what Orca enforces and what a bot is only told, read Security.

More templates

Orca ships 24 templates. The ones above, plus:

TemplateWhat it doesWhen it reports
Bug report intakeDrafts a Linear issue with repro steps for each bug posted in a Slack channel, and files it once you approveWhen a bug is reported
GitHub issue triageProposes labels and duplicates, and lists missing details, for each new issueWhen an issue is opened
Support triageLists unanswered questions in the Slack channels you pick, and notes which already have a Linear issueWeekdays, every 2 hours from 9 AM to 5 PM
Feedback digestGroups feedback from Slack into themes, with quotes and countsMondays at 9 AM
Backlog cleanupLists stale issues, likely duplicates and issues with no ownerThe 1st of each month
Dependency updatesLists outdated and vulnerable packages, most urgent first; one pull request per group once you agreeMondays at 9 AM
Security checkChecks merged changes for leaked secrets, injection, missing auth and unsafe defaultsMondays at 9 AM, and when a pull request merges
Release readinessKeeps a short list of failing checks, pull requests waiting for review and open blockersWeekday mornings
Incident notesKeeps a timeline in an incident channel, then drafts a blameless write-upDuring and after an incident
On-call handoffSummarizes the week's alerts and incidents, and lists open follow-upsFridays at 4 PM
Replies you oweFinds questions to you with no reply in Slack and Linear, and things you said you'd doDaily at 4 PM
Email digestLists emails that need a reply from you; never sends emailWeekdays at 9 AM and 2 PM
Meeting prepLists the issues, pull requests and docs a meeting is about15 minutes before each meeting
Project briefingWrites what changed, what's blocked and what needs a decisionFridays at 3 PM
Spec draftingTurns a Slack thread or doc into a one-page specWhen you ask
Unreviewed agent workLists finished agent work with no review or pull request yetWeekdays at 9 AM
Workspace cleanupLists done or abandoned workspaces; removes only the ones you pickFridays at 4 PM

Write your own

A good first ask names four things:

  1. When: a schedule ("every weekday at 9"), an event ("when a check fails in acme/web") or just "now".
  2. What to do: one job, in a sentence.
  3. Where to report: the bot's chat, a Slack channel, or the issue or pull request it was asked on.
  4. What needs you: what it should ask about before it acts.

For example: "When a pull request is opened in acme/web, check it has a test for each fix. Tell me in #eng which ones don't. Don't comment on GitHub."

Next steps

  • Bots quickstart: create a bot and give it its first job.
  • Connectors: connect Slack, GitHub, Linear, Google and webhooks.
  • Security: what a bot can reach, and who can ask it.
